## Phase 1 — Foundation (engine, crates & architecture)

1. [ ] Update all external crates that need updating for the entire project - See previous release notes for examples of such crates. Ensure CI/CD is also updated for the new crate versions. Exit criteria: cargo check clean on all feature combos, cargo deny check clean, CI green on a scratch branch. worker pins, external crates, ci/cd must all be included in this. LunarWing crates must be bumped from 2.1.6 to 2.1.7 and all crates which require a version bump must also be bumped such as channel wasms. There should not be any 2.1.6 LunarWing crates left. Refer to goals doc from aragents/kimil00p/v2.1.4.0 in order to be thorough. all LunarWing-owned release packages, workspace crates, XMPP bridge, and WASM channels must be updated and lockfiles refreshed. All channel registry packages need bump. A tracked-manifest/lock audit must confirm no project-owned package or path dependency remains at 2.1.6 - External crates audit and update must be completed - ic/vendor/ must be regenerated - Worker/CI audit must be completed - cargo deny check and all associated things usually done with each release should be done. there may be docs guidance that you need to update. See older releases for more info on this if any clarification is needed. External worker upgrades such as kimi, opencode2, pebble, etc may be done in a seperate goal item. Plugins default-export `{id, setup}` (promise API; `Plugin.define` is the identity function, so the plain object is equivalent and stays dependency-free); `setup` registers tools through `ctx.tool.transform` (`packages/plugin/src/promise/tool.ts`); configured plugin paths must be **directories** — a file path in `plugins` is dropped with "configured plugin path must be a directory" (`core/src/config/plugin/source.ts`) — and `Host.resolve` picks up the directory's `index.ts`; tools without `options.codemode: false` go into the codemode execute-inventory instead of the direct definitions (`core/src/tool.ts` snapshot); `Tool.Context` carries `sessionID`; a thrown Error becomes a `Tool.Error` carrying its message (the promise adapter's `toolError`); `ctx.session.get({sessionID})` is an in-process authenticated call returning the unwrapped info (`title`/`parentID` optional); and plain JSON Schema is an accepted `Tool.ValueSchema` — effect 4's `SchemaRepresentation` type-checks declared properties and enforces `required` while **preserving undeclared properties** (probed empirically with effect@4 on this host), so `gitlab_tool`'s free-form action payload rides through. Declared schemas carry types only (no bounds/enums), keeping the plugin a permissive superset of what the hub accepts; the hub stays authoritative.

    - **Why not the kimi MCP-shim shape:** verified against the pinned opencode source (`cb7d8b2f`, v1.18.25) that `opencode serve` owns MCP servers per project *Instance* (`InstanceState.make` in `packages/opencode/src/mcp/index.ts`), spawned once with the serve process's frozen env — a per-task `LUNARWING_TOOL_PROXY_SOCKET` env can never reach an MCP child, and one Instance's MCP tools are shared by every session in the directory. So the opencode lane gets the same bridge-side relay plus a serve-process **plugin** instead of a shim.
    - **Relay + bridge wiring:** `opencode4lunarwing/scripts/lunarwing_tool_proxy.ts` ports the kimi relay unchanged in behavior (same NDJSON IPC shapes, `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}` identifiers, locally pre-checked 256 KiB `tool_call` cap, never-throw startTask/closeTask per EF-2, dropHubSocket per EF-9/EV-4) onto bun-style `.ts` imports; `lunarwing_runtime.ts` gains the ported `isRecord`/`utf8ByteLength`/`truncateUtf8`/`serializeEnvelopeWithinLimit` helpers. `lunarwing_bridge.ts` routes hub `tool_result`/`tool_catalog` frames to the relay, starts the per-task socket inside the task `try`, closes it in `finally`, and drops waiters on hub-socket close in both roles.
    - **The plugin** (`scripts/lunarwing_tool_plugin.ts`, registered by absolute path in `config/opencode.json`): a v1 file plugin (default export `{id, server}` per the pinned `plugin/shared.ts` loader) statically registers the phase-1 set as `lunarwing_memory_read`, `lunarwing_memory_write`, `lunarwing_knowledge_search`, `lunarwing_knowledge_submit_note`, `lunarwing_knowledge_get_topic`, `lunarwing_gitlab_tool` and the legacy `lunarwing_gitlab-tool`. Each `execute()` resolves the calling session's task id from its title (`lunarwing-opencode-<task_id>`, set by the executor) via the plugin host's authenticated client (v1 SDK `session.get({path:{id}})`), caches first-title-wins per session id (a mid-run self-rename cannot redirect calls), and relays `{op:"call"}` over the task's socket; success returns the output text, denied/error/transport failures throw a tool error carrying the hub's sanitized reason (the mapping the kimi shim applies via MCP `isError`). Dependency-free (no `@opencode-ai/plugin`/zod in the runtime image): plain `{description, args, execute}` definitions take the registry's legacy JSON-Schema path (`registry.ts: fromPlugin` passes args through unvalidated), so only hub-required arguments are declared and optional ones are documented in the description — hub validation stays authoritative. Statically visible without a grant, unlike the kimi lane's filtered catalog; the hub's deny-by-default reply is the error text.

The shim now discards up to the terminating newline — keeping the head and a rolling 256-char tail — and answers exactly once with `-32600` carrying the id recovered from the line's top-level edges (id-first `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":N,` or SDK order `…"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":N}`; never a nested `"id"`).     - **EF-1 (Medium, the blocker — relay sockets as cross-task authority bridge):** `ExternalWorkerManager::new` now strips a `mode = "cron"` worker's worker-level `tool_allowlist` with an `error!` at load, so a cron worker container never holds more than `cron.tool_allowlist` (a tick could otherwise borrow a concurrent interactive job's grant through that task's same-uid relay socket). Ordinary dispatches to cron-mode workers now get no proxied tools; the settings-layer warn text, the design-doc TOML example (worker-level list removed, strip documented), and the `ordinary_dispatch_on_cron_worker_still_uses_worker_allowlist` test (rewritten as `cron_worker_worker_level_allowlist_is_stripped_at_load`, asserting denial) were updated to match. Docs now state one worker container is one authority domain across concurrent AND successive jobs, and that per-user isolation needs per-user workers (design doc + WORKER-CONTAINERS.md).
    - **EF-2 (the other required fix):** `startTask` wraps its `mkdirSync`/`rmSync` and returns `null` (task runs tool-less) instead of throwing; `closeTask`'s `rmSync` is best-effort; the bridge's `startTask` call moved inside the `try` so no throw can leak the concurrency slot/workspace lock or leave the hub waiting on a `task_result`. Pinned by two relay unit tests (dir-as-file start, ENOTDIR close) and a bridge test (`a sabotaged tool-proxy directory neither fails the task nor leaks its slot`) that runs the real bridge with the proxy dir replaced by a plain file.
    - **EF-3:** new `cron_allowlist_is_sanitized_at_manager_load` integration test — a cron worker on `ws://127.0.0.2` (loopback-routed by the OS, non-loopback per the strict host check) has its listed `knowledge_search` grant denied on a tick (the cleartext strip is load-only, so deleting the manager's sanitize call fails this test); a loopback phase pins `"*"` not honored and `"shell"` dropped while a genuine grant round-trips.

12. [x] work on https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/work_items/332 - last attempt was rejected, see comments on MR: https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/merge_requests/1528
    - DONE (asks 1-2; ask 3 explicitly deferred) 2026-10-12 (Rarity, item-12, branch `sweetiebot/kimi-12-20261012-0128`): re-attempt of the closed MR !1528 (run-67 review verdict "REQUEST CHANGES"; DJ-1..DJ-6 all addressed in-tree). WI#332 remains open pending the owner's acceptance of the ask-3 deferral.
    - **DJ-1 (the blocker — authentication-fallback regression):** `gate_outlives_thread` exempts gates with `action_name == "authentication_fallback"` + `original_message: Some(..)` from the waiting-thread liveness rule in the predicate, the 60 s sweep, and boot-time `reconcile_pending_gate_state` (repairing the pre-existing restart inconsistency noted in the review): such gates are resolved by re-dispatching the original message, never by resuming the thread, and live until expiry exactly as before !1528. Tests: `auth_fallback_gate_outlives_its_terminated_thread` (survives sweep + listing filter + boot reconcile) and `engine_pending_gate_lookups_return_fallback_gates_for_dead_threads` (both HTTP-facing lookups return the gate, so `auth-token`/`auth-cancel` no longer 409 and `loadPendingGates` keeps the card).
    - **DJ-2 (replacement-gate race):** new `PendingGateStore::discard_if_request` compares the stored request id under the key reservation + index lock, and the sweep uses it — a replacement gate parked under the same key while the liveness read was in flight wins (same guarantee the expiry path's `run_if_absent` provides). Tests: two store-level pins (`test_discard_if_request_removes_only_the_matching_gate`, `test_discard_if_request_protects_a_replacement_gate`) and `dead_thread_sweep_never_discards_a_replacement_gate` on the blocking thread-load rig.
    - **DJ-3 (fail-closed resolution):** `resolve_gate` checks liveness right after `take_verified`; an approval that raced a thread termination no longer executes the gated action — the caller gets a "thread ended" reply and the gateway a `gate_resolved`/`thread_terminated` broadcast (the gate was already taken out of the store, so it is gone for good).
    - **DJ-4:** a persistence failure during a sweep discard is now warn-logged with the error AND the gate is still audited/broadcast as discarded, because the in-memory entry is already gone — no more silent unaudited removal with an orphaned prompt.
    - **DJ-5/DJ-6:** ENGINE-V2.md records the real contract (fallback exemption, request-id-checked sweep, fail-closed resolve race, one-sweep-interval visibility bound for already-delivered SSE cards); the agent_loop warn labels `conversation_scope()` as `scope`; `get_engine_pending_gate` filters liveness BEFORE newest-first selection so a dead newest gate cannot hide an older live one (`engine_pending_gate_lookup_skips_a_dead_newest_gate`).
    - **Ask 1:** `GET /api/chat/pending-gates` and the single-gate lookup drop gates whose thread vanished/left `Waiting`/changed owner; the 60 s sweep discards them from memory + persistence, warn-logs user/thread/request/gate/action ids, and broadcasts `thread_terminated` so open prompts clear. **Ask 2:** approvals on engine-routed channels that match no engine gate fall through to v1 with a warn naming user/channel/request id/scope (ids only, no args).
    - **Ask 3 (NOT re-run — explicitly deferred per the run-67 verdict's "done or explicitly deferred"):** re-running the WI#320 external-worker gate scenario needs the live worker harness and the original evidence was lost with the purged host; the new warn logs on the resolve/sweep/fallthrough paths now capture exactly the request ids/thread ids ask 3 wants on the next live run. Same deferral shape as item 6's audio-capture deferral; pending the WI owner's acceptance.

13. [x] work on https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/work_items/334 - last attempt rejected (see comments in closed MR and address if necessary) https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/merge_requests/1536
    - DONE 2026-10-12 (Rarity, item-13, branch `rarity/kimi-13-20261012-0603`): re-attempt of the closed MR !1536 (review run-70 note #7613 verdict "REQUEST CHANGES (narrow): DV-1 … APPROVE WITH NITS once it lands"). The !1536 head `f0c3073e5` is merged onto target `c85ee5be9` (merge `2198d1735`, conflict-only goals resolution, this file). The review itself verified the fix works in every pebble spawn mode (real dash simulation); the open findings DV-1..DV-8 were wiring/tests/wording and are all addressed in-tree.
    - **DV-1 (Medium, the blocker — smoke could probe a different container store than the build and kill a good fat build):** mt-admin now passes its runtime explicitly (`CONTAINER_RT="$CONTAINER_RT" "$smoke_script"`, `05-tenant-build.sh`) and the smoke honours `${CONTAINER_RT:-}` before its podman-first autodetect (unknown values exit 2). Both image-mode legs run `--pull=never`, so the gate can never fall back to a registry pull of the short name. Fat builds now target `lunarwing-worker-pebble:candidate` and re-tag `:latest` only after the probe passes (then drop the candidate tag): a failed smoke can no longer leave the failing image as `:latest` (the review's "gate does not protect the image" sub-finding). Harness pins: runtime passthrough for both docker and podman, candidate-only probing, exactly one tag+rmi per fat build, slim builds untouched, and a failing-stub case asserting the build dies and `:latest` is never re-tagged.
    - **DV-3 (Low, ride-along):** the fat RUN block pre-creates `/root/go/bin`, so the existence-guarded `${GOPATH}/bin` restore holds from the very first task command (`go install x && x` in one command) instead of only after the directory appears.
    - **DV-2 (Low):** the `env -i` leg is now honestly described as proving *resolution*, not function (a stripped-env `cargo` cannot re-locate `rustc` for its own subprocesses; pebble never spawns that mode) — smoke PASS line and header, Dockerfile comment, PEBBLE-WORKER.md, and the WI#320 F6 note all reworded.
    - **DV-4 (Low — mutation gaps):** committed `SmokeScriptStubRuntimeTests` run the real smoke script against stub podman/docker binaries, pinning argv (`--pull=never --network=none`), runtime choice (env override beats autodetect, unknown runtime exits 2), and failure propagation (failing runtime → smoke exit 1 after exactly one leg — catches a never-failing `run_leg`). `DockerfileAnchorRunBlockTests` extract the anchors RUN block from the Dockerfile and execute it **verbatim under dash** against a stub toolchain tree at the real absolute paths: the happy path asserts the symlinks/wrapper exist (catches a never-true guard) and that leg 1 resolves cargo via the rustup proxies and go via the GOROOT restore while leg 2 resolves via `/usr/local/bin`; a sabotaged stub asserts loud failure (catches `|| true` on a leg).
    - **DV-5 (Low/Info — fat proof ran nowhere in CI):** closed by the same execution test — it runs as root in the ci-config image (`python:3.12-slim-bookworm`, dash as `/bin/sh`), so the verbatim fat-image proof now executes in every ci-config run without a separate job; it skips cleanly on non-root/non-dash hosts.
    - **DV-6 (Info — doc claim false for go):** fixed at the root instead of by rewording — the snippet now prepends in reverse priority order and restores the exact image ENV PATH order (rustup proxies, then `GOROOT/bin`, then `GOPATH/bin`). Login shells resolve `go` through the snippet's GOROOT restore, so "only a sanitised non-login shell resolves through the symlinks" is now true for all four tools, and the build proof's `/bin/sh -lc` leg actually exercises the GOROOT restore (verified in the end-to-end smoke below).
    - **DV-7 (Info):** all PATH edits go through a helper that skips non-absolute or non-existent homes and never introduces an empty PATH entry (relative/empty `CARGO_HOME`/`GOROOT`/`GOPATH`, or an empty PATH, could otherwise put the current directory on PATH); the helper is `unset -f` after use and the snippet stays dash-clean. New behavioural regressions for both cases.
    - **DV-8 (Info):** "gauntlet smoke check" wording removed from PEBBLE-WORKER.md, the F6 note, and this trail (no gauntlet lane invokes the script; it is an mt-admin build gate). `LUNARWING_PEBBLE_TOOLCHAIN_SMOKE` is now documented at its use site as a test-only hook. All probe output (image and container mode) is captured and piped through `cat -v`, so image-controlled stdout cannot spray terminal escapes at the operator.

    - **Security scope (MR !1535 review run 70, DU-1):** the checks stop partial forgeries (only the stamp or `thread_history` edited) and store corruption. They do not stop a full `mission.json` forge: setting `user_id` to the victim or a shared sentinel (`__shared__`, legacy `system`), `project_id` to the victim thread's project and adding the victim thread to `thread_history` passes all four checks, because every mission-side value compared comes from the forged record (PoC executed in the review). WI#322's write-path reservation is the fix; a mission id stamped on the thread row (under `engine/.runtime`) would be the unforgeable side
    - `resolve_stamped_interruption` now mirrors `record_restart_interrupted_thread`'s parity checks — `thread_type == Mission`, `project_id` match, owner match for non-shared missions — plus `mission.thread_history.contains(&thread_id)`; on mismatch the stamp is cleared with a `warn!` and no retry marker or notification is produced (for a partial forgery; see the security scope above).
    - Same scan-time parity check applied in `recover_pending_mission_retries` and `resume_recoverable_threads`, which trusted `thread_history` blindly (both already checked thread type; project/owner checks added, warn+skip on mismatch).
    - Tests: stamps naming a foreign user's thread, a non-Mission thread, and an own thread absent from `thread_history` each assert the stamp is cleared with no marker write and no notification; retry recovery over a planted foreign history thread asserts no retry arms and the foreign marker is untouched. `cargo test -p lunarwing_engine --lib -- boot_resolution retry_recovery_skips`: 10/10 pass (includes the 6 pre-existing boot-resolution tests); `cargo clippy -p lunarwing_engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean
    - WI#322's `engine/projects/**` write-path reservation deliberately not landed here: it is labeled `2.1.7.0` (next release), appears in no item of this goals file, and the WI#338 cross-link comment (#7413) recommends landing #322 first or in the same pass, after which these checks defend against residual or legacy forged records. Until then they are partial protection only

    - **AE-3 (single-slot stamp shadows overlapping fires):** `pending_shutdown_interruption` is now a map keyed by thread id (`insert_shutdown_interruption_stamp` / `stamped_interrupted_threads` / per-thread `clear_shutdown_interruption_stamp`), so the cron-tick + armed-retry overlap keeps a stamp per unsettled fire and the boot sweep resolves each. The pre-WI#339 single-slot shape (`{"thread_id": ..., "recorded_at": ...}`) is still read — the WI#338 forgery tests write it verbatim and now double as legacy coverage — and is folded into the map on the next write.
    - **AE-4 (one bad row aborts the sweep):** `resolve_pending_shutdown_interruptions` isolates per mission (re-load failure → `warn!` + `continue`, stamp kept for next boot) and per stamp within a mission (resolution error → `warn!`, other stamps still resolve); the router's failure log for the sweep is promoted `debug!` → `warn!`.
    - **AE-5 (non-retry outcome notices owed at shutdown lost):** `process_mission_outcome_and_notify` stamps `pending_outcome_notification` (map keyed by thread id: response/is_error/recorded_at) when the notice is owed but undelivered and no retry marker exists to carry the `notified` flag (completion, MaxIterations, budget exhaustion, exhausted retry chain). New `deliver_pending_outcome_notifications` boot sweep emits the notices once the bridge consumer is wired (called from `router.rs` after the WI#276 resolution pass), with the stamp removal as the acknowledgement — the same best-effort semantics as WI#301/WI#276 (a crash between send and save repeats the notice at the next boot). `send_outcome_notification` now borrows the response (`Option<&str>`).
    - **Tests (8 new, in the mission test module):** the AE-3 two-fire scenario end-to-end (both stamps kept, both resolved — markers, notices, cleared stamps), legacy-stamp fold-on-write, AE-4 per-mission isolation via `failing_mission_loads` (corrupt row blocks only itself, next boot resolves it), AE-4 per-stamp isolation via a new `TestStore::failing_thread_loads` hook, AE-5 completion notice stamped + delivered once (no repeat on second sweep), AE-5 MaxIterations error notice, AE-5 negative (a retried interruption rides the marker's `notified` flag — no stamp), AE-5 no-channels (nothing owed, nothing stamped).
    - **Verification:** `cargo check -p lunarwing_engine --tests` clean; `cargo test -p lunarwing_engine --lib` 728/728 pass (mission module 167/167 incl. all pre-existing WI#276/WI#301/WI#338 tests unchanged in behavior); `cargo clippy -p lunarwing_engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo clippy --lib -- -D warnings` (covers `router.rs`) clean; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean.
    - **Merged onto item 14 (!1556, WI#337) on 2026-10-12** (run-79 review #7898 EN-X / #7897 EO-X):
      - **Kept from !1556:** the already-owned guards (marker, `Running`, superseded) and the stamp-first write order.
      - **Kept from this MR:** the per-stamp loop with warn-and-continue.
      - **Stamp age:** `recorded_at` is now read per thread from the map entry, with the legacy top-level fallback. A naive merge read only the top-level value, which marked every map stamp stale.
      - **Overlapping fires, newest wins:** only the newest stamped fire earns the retry; the superseded older fire is resolved record-only. This changes the AE-3 two-fire test above from "both retried".
      - **Rollback:** a stamp whose resolution fails is restored in memory before the mission's next stamp resolves.
      - **Admission:** armed retries are collected and admitted into `recovered_retries` after the fire guard drops.
      - **Tests:** 2 new tests (per-thread stale map entry; failed stamp kept when a sibling saves). The !1556 DB-1b test now asserts the isolated `Ok` result
      - **Verification:** `cargo test -p lunarwing_engine --lib runtime::mission` passes 177/177. Removing the rollback, or reading only the top-level `recorded_at`, fails 1 and 8 tests respectively.

    - **EM-1 (the blocker — the capture could not contain the crash line):** verified against the real pinned source (fetched tag `v1.18.25` → resolves to exactly `OPENCODE_REF` `cb7d8b2f5e44876ef98b661dc10590c915af3a9f`): `opencode serve` at v1.18.25 has NO per-command log flags; `--print-logs`/`--log-level` are root yargs options setting `OPENCODE_PRINT_LOGS`/`OPENCODE_LOG_LEVEL`; `loggers()` (packages/core/src/observability/logging.ts:68) returns `[fileLogger(), stderrLogger]` only when `OPENCODE_PRINT_LOGS === "1"`, else `[fileLogger()]` writing to `Global.Path.log/opencode.log` = `$XDG_DATA_HOME|~/.local/share/opencode/log/opencode.log`; the 500 middleware (server/routes/instance/httpapi/middleware/error.ts:30) logs the cause via `Effect.logError("failed", { ref, error, cause })` through that logger and answers with `message: "Unexpected server error…", ref: "err_<8 hex>"`. So the entrypoint tee (removed — serve invocation reverted to the pre-!1554 shape) could never see the crash line; the enrichment now reads the structured log instead. Bonus: the 500 body's `ref` (preserved by the SDK error wrapper under `cause.body.data.ref`) is extracted (`opencodeErrorRef`) and used for exact per-request correlation
    - **EM-2 (lone-surrogate frame drop):** the per-line cap is now by code points (`Array.from(line).slice(0, 400)`), so an astral character can never be split into a lone surrogate that would make the hub's serde_json reject the whole `task_result` and hang the job for the worker timeout. Pinned by a test placing an astral char exactly at code point 399 plus a well-formedness sweep of every attached line.
    - **EM-3 (unbounded, following read):** the log is opened `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, gated on `fstat().isFile()`, and read as one bounded ≤8 KiB window (`readSync` loop capped at TAIL_BYTES). A planted FIFO cannot wedge the task (nonblocking open + isFile reject; size-0 FIFO yields an empty window), a symlink to `/proc/<pid>/environ` or `/dev/zero` dies at `O_NOFOLLOW`, and a renamed-over directory fails `readSync` with EISDIR. The `isFile` gate is defense-in-depth: planting a device node needs CAP_MKNOD, which the same-uid attacker lacks (the one mutation-test survivor — unkillable without privileges — is documented in the test commit)
    - **EM-4 (cross-task/user leak):** the executor records the structured-log size at task start (`serveLogStartSize()`), and the window reads only from that offset; when the failing response carries a `ref`, only that request's `level=Error` line attaches (fail-closed: ref present but its line never lands ⇒ nothing attaches). One leading byte before the window disambiguates a mid-line cut (partial dropped) from a clean boundary (kept) — found and fixed by the new offset test itself.
    - **EM-5 (secret scrub):** only `level=Error` lines are eligible, and known secret values (bridge credential envs: `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, `TENSORZERO_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `AGENT_AUTH_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `PASEO_TOKEN`, plus this task's injected `context.environment` values; ≥8 chars, ≤64 values) are replaced with `[redacted]` before the tail is returned into the job record.
    - **EM-6 (writability/false-capture):** with the tee gone there is no entrypoint capture to probe and no stale-file attach: `OPENCODE_SERVE_LOG` is a path override whose explicit empty value disables enrichment outright (no default-path fallback); unset resolves the xdg structured-log default. The structured log itself is serve-owned (append, no rotation) in the compose `opencode-data` volume — documented for operators.
    - **EM-7 (mutations, path freeze, entrypoint):** the path is resolved once at module load (`SERVE_LOG_PATH`), so a per-task credential grant named `OPENCODE_SERVE_LOG` cannot retarget or disable enrichment (`resolveServeLogPath` exported and unit-pinned; a subprocess pin proves a post-load env mutation has no effect). The test suite kills all four previously-surviving mutations (`.slice(-TAIL_LINES)` dropped, whole-file read, `TAIL_LINES=1000`, `TAIL_BYTES=1 MiB` — each bound now pinned independently) plus the EM-2 surrogate cap, the EM-3 O_NOFOLLOW, the EM-4 offset and ref filter, the EM-5 level filter, and the EM-7 per-call-env read (10 of 11 mutations dead; the isFile-gate drop is documented-unreachable, see EM-3). There is no entrypoint change anymore (the tee was the only one), so the "entrypoint change has no repo test" gap is moot; `bash -n` + shellcheck clean (only pre-existing SC2016 infos in the untouched git-credential block).

Pin in `test-proxy-openrc-transition.sh` (per the review's prototype): a subshell defines `python3() { :; }`, clears `_OPENRC_PROXY_PYTHON3_RESOLVED`/`_OPENRC_PROXY_PYTHON3_RESOLVED_FROM` and `_OPENRC_PROXY_UNIT_SAFE_CACHE` so the resolution really re-runs, and asserts the safe unit still passes. The "refused once" overstatement is corrected to the review's wording: units whose interpreter spelling differs from the canonical one are refused until re-rendered, with `upgrade-tenant` doing this automatically via `render_all_openrc_proxy_units` in `upgrade_tenant_locked` (`04-tenant-lifecycle.sh`; cited by function, not the drifting line anchor).

The checker extracts the port from the unit (strict prefix + `[0123456789]{1,5}` under `LC_ALL=C`, HF-6) and compares the file byte-for-byte against a fresh render with `cmp -s` — which also covers NUL/CR, the final newline and trailing lines, so the old byte-hygiene pass is gone. The PATH-flip shape now yields rc 0 with no archive growth; a unit rendered under a *different* resolution (e.g. pre-pin fleets, or after the override changes) is refused once and `render-units --all-proxies` repairs it — documented in `docs/ops/MULTITENANCY-PRODUCTION.md` per the must-do's release line. The fast path runs the fork-heavy validations only on a miss (HU-4 correction: not fork-free — the `$(stat …)` key read forks on every hit, ~4.3 ms per hit against ~3.7 ms for a full check at the target). 63. [x] work on https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/work_items/364 - last attempt was on https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/merge_requests/1707 please see MR comments and address findings
    - DONE 2026-10-17 (SweetieBot, item-63, branch `sweetiebot/kimi-63-20261017-1905`): re-attempt of the closed MR !1707 (run-143 review note #9723, verdict "REQUEST CHANGES (narrow)"). The !1707 head `3d948027a` was never merged (closed unmerged), so the full WI#364 implementation is re-applied on the current target with **all four run-143 findings fixed in-tree**: JL-1 (Low), JL-2 (Low), JL-3 (Low), JL-4 (Info). WI#364's proposed **Option 1 (orchestrator pre-injection)** — the Level 1.5 escalation — is implemented; the prompt-level rule had saturated across threads #12/#16/#22.
    - **Implementation (WI#364):** new `inject_missing_imports` helper in `ic/crates/lunarwing_engine/src/executor/scripting.rs` scans LLM code blocks for dotted references (`module.`) to the six Monty-available stdlib modules (`asyncio`, `json`, `re`, `math`, `datetime`, `sys`) and prepends `import <module>` for any that are referenced, not already imported, and not called as a bare function. Wired into `execute_code_with_skills_and_action_limit` — the single choke point every LLM-generated block passes through before `MontyRun::new` (direct CodeAct steps, `__execute_code_step__` from the orchestrator, `rlm_query` children). Explicit imports take precedence; import-clean code executes byte-identically.
    - **JL-1 fix (injected `import json` broke the builtin `json` tool):** new `calls_bare_name` check — a block that calls `json(...)` as a bare function (the builtin tool, `ic/src/tools/registry.rs` `PROTECTED_TOOL_NAMES`) never gets an injected `import json` that would rebind the name to the module. Pinned by `inject_missing_imports_ignores_json_called_as_a_bare_function` (unit) and `json_tool_call_survives_a_dotted_json_mention` (integration, MockEffects `json` action, dotted mention in a comment): the tool call round-trips where the !1707 head failed with `TypeError: object is not callable`.
    - **JL-2 fix (injected lines shifted every reported error position):** `execute_code_with_skills_and_action_limit` is split into a wrapper (injection + position fixup) and `execute_prepared_code` (the original body compiling the prepared code). After a had-error run with injection, the wrapper subtracts the injected line count from `File "step.py", line N` and the prefix length from `at byte range N..N` in the executor-appended error tail (`shift_numbers_after` / `push_shifted_number`); output the block printed itself is untouched. Pinned by `injected_imports_do_not_shift_reported_line_numbers` (a ZeroDivisionError on line 2 and a SyntaxError on line 2 both report `line 2` with the block-relative byte range).
    - **JL-3 fix (`from asyncio import ...` / `import asyncio as aio` wrongly suppressed injection):** `already_imports` now counts only imports that bind the bare module name — `import module`, `import module.sub`, multi-name `import a, b` (plain name or alias matching the module), indented imports. From-imports (`from module import x` binds `x`) and aliases (`import module as alias` binds `alias`) no longer suppress injection, so `from asyncio import gather` + `asyncio.gather(...)` now runs. Pinned by `inject_missing_imports_ignores_from_and_alias_bindings` (both shapes inject `import asyncio`).
    - **JL-4 fix (unpinned word boundary + comment-tail strip):** `references_module`'s leading word-boundary check now reads the byte from the whole code (a skipped match no longer resets it), and two new pins kill the previously-surviving K6/K3 mutants: `inject_missing_imports_respects_the_leading_word_boundary` (`my_asyncio.gather(1)` does not inject) and `inject_missing_imports_ignores_names_in_a_comment_tail` (`import os  # , json` counts as importing only os, so `json.` later still injects). The `def`-scope limitation (an `import` inside a `def` does not bind the name at top level) is recorded as a known limitation in the helper docstring per the review.
    - **Preamble updated for the root cause:** the preamble's own `asyncio.gather()` example carried `import asyncio` while real blocks omitted it; `prompts/codeact_preamble.md` now drops the import line from the example and documents auto-injection in the Parallel-execution and Runtime-environment sections. `MONTY.md` documents the Level 1.5 behavior, the three guards, and the error position correction.
    - **Mutation verification on this tree:** each review fix was mutation-verified — removing the `calls_bare_name` check fails exactly `json_tool_call_survives_a_dotted_json_mention`; disabling the position fixup fails exactly `injected_imports_do_not_shift_reported_line_numbers`; reverting `already_imports` to the !1707 shape fails exactly `inject_missing_imports_ignores_from_and_alias_bindings`; dropping the word-boundary check fails exactly `inject_missing_imports_respects_the_leading_word_boundary`; dropping the comment-tail strip fails exactly `inject_missing_imports_ignores_names_in_a_comment_tail`. Restored tree green after every mutation.
    - **Tests:** 16 new tests (11 helper unit + 4 integration incl. the review's three pins + the end-to-end `asyncio_gather_without_import_succeeds_via_pre_injection` — the WI's exact primary scenario: `results = await asyncio.gather(echo(), echo())` with no `import asyncio` executes both tool calls and returns the combined answer).
    - **Verification on this tree:** `cargo check -p lunarwing_engine` clean; `cargo test -p lunarwing_engine --lib scripting` **71/71**; `cargo clippy -p lunarwing_engine --lib --tests -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt -p lunarwing_engine -- --check` clean. Engine-crate-local change set (scripting.rs + the embedded preamble) — no daemon/bridge/worker code touched.
    - **Known limitation (accepted, documented in helper docstring and MONTY.md):** the scan is a line/boundary matcher, not a full Python parser — `module.` inside a string literal triggers a harmless extra `import` (a no-op at runtime), and an `import module` inside a `def` does not bind the name at top level (and does not suppress injection, so the top-level reference still gets the injected import and works).

65. [x] work on https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/work_items/371
    - DONE 2026-10-18 (Rarity, item-65, branch `aj/kimi-65-20261018-0100`): WI#371 (DN-5 from the MR !1531 review) fully implemented — cron worker control persistence now fails closed on read errors and preserves breaker state across restarts. All four acceptance criteria addressed in-tree:
    - **AC1 (fail-closed reads):** `load_mission_state` distinguishes absent rows (clean defaults) from failed reads/parse failures — a control-state or legacy pause-key read/parse failure starts the mission **paused** with an actionable `durability_error` (names the fault and the remedy), logged at `error!` on startup; the tick counter stays deliberately lenient (a reused index never double-fires, warn-logged). The legacy `cron-mission:<id>:paused` key remains a read-only fallback so pre-WI#371 pauses stay paused.
    - **AC2 (durable breaker counter):** pause flag + `consecutive_abandoned` now share one atomic settings row `cron-mission:<id>:control` (`{"paused", "consecutive_abandoned"}` — single `set_setting` is the atomic unit; the supervisor is the row's only writer, so no CAS needed). The counter persists on every abandon, the trip lands pause+count in one write, and it resets durably on owner resume or a resolved tick (reset skipped when already 0, so healthy ticks cost no write).
    - **AC3 (retrying writes + honest reporting):** control-state writes retry with bounded exponential backoff (4 attempts, 100/200/400 ms, ~700 ms worst case inside the DQ-4 10 s control budget). Exhaustion never claims success: `MissionState.durability_error` (sticky until the next successful write) surfaces via `CronMissionStatus.durability_error` → the HTTP route answers with top-level `"durable": false` and the `cron_mission` tool returns the field; `cron_paused`/`cron_resumed` audit rows carry a `durable` flag. The DQ-4 enqueue/202 contract is unchanged.
    - **AC4 (fault-injection tests, shared DB abstraction):** new `FaultingSettingsDb` test decorator (`ic/src/db/recording.rs`, backend-agnostic over the `Database` trait — PostgreSQL-portable, exercised on libsql) fails prefixed reads outright and a programmed number of writes, recording every attempt. Unit pins: absent/malformed/failed load distinction incl. legacy fallback, retry-then-success (3 attempts, fault cleared, row content), exhaustion (exactly 4 attempts, fault recorded), after_tick row content for abandon→trip→reset. Integration pins: read-failure mission starts paused and never fires (every-second schedule, 3 s negative window), heal+resume recovers durably; pause write exhaustion is reported and no row pretends success, healed resume persists; breaker counter survives supervisor restarts between abandons 1/2 → trip at 3 → still tripped+counter 3 after another restart (daily schedule, manual fires only). The DN-3 gateway test now asserts the control row, the `durable` flag, and that the legacy key is never rewritten.
    - **Verification on this tree:** `cargo test --lib cron_worker` 24/24 (5 new), `cargo test --test external_worker_integration cron_` **31/31** (3 new + the reworked DN-3 gateway test), `cargo clippy --lib --tests -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --all` applied, `check_no_panics.py` and `check-boundaries.sh` pass. Design doc (`EXTERNAL-WORKER-CRON-MODE.md`) and `NETWORK_SECURITY.md` updated: fail-closed reads, atomic row, retry budget, `durable` reporting, and the rollback note (a downgrade would not see pauses written by this build — the legacy key is read-only now)
    - **Note:** WI#371 stays open for maintainer acceptance (same shape as other WI close-outs); the in-tree work is complete.

Each gate mutation-verified independently (neutering 09 fails the imperative pin; neutering 07 fails the quadlet pin). The harness's transient-failure simulation now makes the origin repo unreachable with its URL unchanged (the old repoint-the-origin trick would now be refused for the right reason). Pin: a rogue repo with a real commit is refused with the origin-mismatch message, writes nothing, and leaves no published manifest; mutation-verified (always-true origin check fails the pin). The stricter operator-configured-root variant (non-default homes only under `dirname $STUDY_NOTES_DEFAULT_HOME`) was considered and NOT taken — it breaks operators who relocate the checkout to any other root-owned path while the root-owned-ancestor rule already confines the location to operator-controlled territory.

    - **WI#450 (MC-1, Low — relocated config-hash sidecars were cross-tenant readable):** `_store_container_hash` (`ic/scripts/mt-admin-lib/00-common.sh`) now forces `$CONTAINER_HASH_DIR` and the per-tenant dir to 0700 after the `mkdir -p` and writes the `.hash` file under `umask 077` (0600) — root's ambient umask was leaving the base 0755, `<tenant>/` 0755 and the file 0644, so any local user (including every other tenant) could read each sidecar. The `mkdir -p` itself stays OUTSIDE the umask 077 subshell per the WI's explicit instruction: on a host where `/var/lib/lunarwing` does not exist yet, a 0700 base would block tenants from reaching `/var/lib/lunarwing/voice-models` (installed 0755). `_store_container_hash` is the only sidecar writer (the other `.hash` sites in 02-containers/07-units-systemd/08-units-openrc/09-worker-config are `rm -f` cleaners and one `cat` reader), so the mode fix covers every sidecar.     - **WI#450 (MC-3, the related Info — remove-tenant left the relocated sidecars behind):** `remove_tenant` (`04-tenant-lifecycle.sh`) now removes `$(_hash_dir "$name")` right after `ports_deallocate` — before the WI#446 relocation `--purge` removed the sidecars along with the home; the relocation silently orphaned them. The name has already been through `sanitize_name`; the `[[ -n "$name" ]]` guard keeps an empty name from resolving to the whole base directory, exactly the WI's suggested fix. This also covers OpenRC tenants, which never went through `uninstall_tenant_systemd` (that path removed only the vision and voice hashes).
    - **WI#451 (MC-2, Low — the voice half of the run-174 LW-1 High had no symlink-redirect pin):** `test-mt-admin-voice.sh` gains the missing pin right after the "fallback removal refuses unmanaged unit" block — a tenant-planted symlink at a parent directory component (on a real host, `~/.config/containers -> /etc/containers/systemd`) must make `render_voice_quadlet` AND `render_voice_systemd_fallback_unit` refuse the render and leave nothing behind the link. The Quadlet arm links `$ROOT/wi446-qparent` and redefines `tenant_quadlet_dir` inside the pin's subshell (this harness keeps the Quadlet dir outside the fixture home; linking the FINAL directory instead would not catch a `mkdir` revert because root's `mkdir -p` on an existing link is a no-op); the fallback arm links `$(tenant_home alpha)/.config/systemd`. The `tenant_quadlet_dir` redefinition happens inside the subshell `wi446_voice_pin` runs the render in, so it does not leak into the rest of the harness.

    - **NP-1 (Low, behaviour and accuracy — fixed):** both premises behind the blanket EINVAL rule were false. On the kimi lane `realpathSync` runs per-component `lstat` then `readlink`, so a task swapping a component from a symlink to a directory raises EINVAL — the DS-2 swap race that must stay transient — and transient failures are bounded by `max_reentries`, not retried forever. EINVAL is back in the transient set in all three TS lanes; the genuinely permanent case (fchown onto a task uid the user namespace does not map) is classified at the `fchown` call by a new `handoffCreatedDir`, which throws `PermanentBootstrapError`. The classifier is exported as `isNonRetryableBootstrapErrno` and the per-source rule is pinned both ways in every lane: `EINVAL` transient, injected fchown `EINVAL` permanent with the unmapped-id message, and a non-EINVAL fchown errno still transient. Pebble needed no classifier change (std maps EINVAL to `InvalidInput`); per run-208 NT-2 its comment now records that glibc realpath(3) has had no lstat→readlink window since 2.33 (the image is Debian 12 / glibc 2.36), so the fchown onto an unmapped id is this lane's only bootstrap EINVAL. The EINVAL change is also labelled a **new** change rather than run-170 LP-16 — LP-16 is the stale text plus the rollback note, which this branch completes.
    - **NP-2 (Low, security hardening — fixed):** the LP-15 probe made root create and unlink a file inside directories it had not verified — `/tmp/lunarwing-tool-proxy` before the foreign-owner check (task-squattable) and the task workspace on every split-mode spawn. `bridge_uid()` now reads the effective uid (second field of the `Uid:` line) from `/proc/self/status`, which is world-readable whatever the process's dumpability — so it also fixes the non-dumpable-/proc/self-owner problem LP-15 was about — and it needs no write and no directory argument. `refuse_shared_identity` and `refuse_foreign_proxy_dir` lost their directory parameter. Pinned by `bridge_uid_reads_self_status_without_writing_anywhere`, which runs the lookup in a child whose `TMPDIR` is read-only (a probe-shaped implementation cannot create its file) and cross-checks the value against the fsuid of a file the process creates. **Mutation-verified:** restoring the probe shape fails exactly that pin.
    - **NP-3 (Low, accuracy — fixed):** the trail no longer calls LP-13 an "ordering note" (it is a fix: chmod before chown, so the mode change runs while root still owns the socket and needs no `CAP_FOWNER`) here, in `WORKER-CONTAINERS.md` or in the release notes. The commit ids cited are this branch's own, and the count is the nine commits above, not the closed branch's. (The "Rarity vs Applejack" half of NP-3 was withdrawn by note #11525: the merged item-72/item-74 trails use the same paired-agent form.)
    - **NP-4 (Info — fixed):** the earlier bullet's claims about `8e6873230` landing via !1882, "seven minutes", and "zero review notes" are gone; nothing in this branch restates them. No LP-7 code or prose-claim remains — the only mentions are the "declined per #11230" lines.

90. [x] work on https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/work_items/384 - see last attempt, comments on closed MR https://git.lunarwing.org/lunarwing/lunarwing2/-/merge_requests/1839 - address MM-1/MM-2-Info
    - DONE 2026-10-21 (SweetieBot, item-90, branch `sweetiebot/kimi-90-20261021-2105`): the `validate_llm_url` die-echo display-hygiene finding is fixed in-tree and the two !1839 run-187 Info findings are addressed — the two (and only two) die sites that interpolate the tenant-controlled URL now render it through `sanitize_display_value`, the helper encodes a literal `%` as `%25` (MM-1a), and the `...` truncation marker is appended only when a byte was actually dropped (MM-1b). MM-2 (label drift) is the maintainer's call; no code change required.
    - **Mechanism covered:** the scheme-gate die (`06-env-generation.sh`, drifted from the WI's :855 to :1000 in the current tree) is the critical site — it fires **before** the whitespace/control-character gate, so a URL carrying raw ANSI escapes or control bytes previously reached root-side terminal output and logs verbatim; the no-host die (:1020) echoes the same value. The other die sites (whitespace/userinfo/`%`/RFC-charset) already describe the violation without echoing the value — verified by grep sweep that no other `die`/`say` site in mt-admin-lib interpolates a raw URL.
    - **Helper:** `sanitize_display_value` (`00-common.sh`, next to `die`) %-encodes every non-printable byte under `LC_ALL=C` (`\x1b[31m` → `%1B[31m`, tab → `%09`, LF → `%0A`) and caps the echo at 160 chars (second arg overrides) with a `...` marker, so a hostile value can neither forge terminal output nor flood logs. A literal `%` is encoded as `%25` so the rendering is unambiguous (a raw `%1B` reads as `%251B`, never as an encoded ESC). The `...` marker fires only when the next iteration would overflow (`i + 1 < n`), so a value that fits exactly under the cap gains no spurious marker. Printable shell metacharacters (`$()`, `&`, `;`) stay verbatim by design: `die` prints them with `printf '%s'` and no path downstream evals die output (the WI#376 sibling-sink sweep), so only the bytes that can forge display are encoded — exactly the WI's "%-encode or truncate" fix shape, both halves.
    - **Touchpoints:** `06-env-generation.sh` (the `validate_llm_url` body itself) covers the `07-units-systemd.sh:768` and `08-units-openrc.sh:586` render callers too — their labels interpolate only `sanitize_name`'d tenant names (`[a-z0-9-]`, no display hazard), and their URLs flow through the same validator. `export-tenant.sh` (checked because the KB note names its local copy) does not echo URLs in die messages.
    - **Tests (8 new pins, `test-add-tenant-env-flags.sh`):** scheme-gate die %-encodes a raw ANSI-escape URL with no raw ESC byte in the captured output; no-host die stays readable for a clean URL; a 400-char URL echo is truncated under the cap; printable `$( )`/`&` survive verbatim (data, not hazards); the helper encodes tab/ESC; the helper honors a custom cap; MM-1a — a literal `%` is encoded as `%25` (`gopher://x/%1B/y` → `...%251B/y`); MM-1b — a 157-char value that fits exactly under the default 160 cap gains no spurious `...` marker. All run in-subshell like the existing bad-shape loop. **Mutation-verified:** reverting the scheme-gate die to the raw `$url` echo fails exactly the escape pin and the oversized-cap pin (2 pins); reverting the `%` guard to leave `%` literal fails exactly the literal-percent pin (1 pin); reverting the `i + 1 < n` check to always append `...` fails exactly the exact-cap pin (1 pin). Each mutant leaves all other pins green.

Shell + harness-only change set (no daemon/bridge/worker Rust, no CI YAML), so no cargo lanes re-run. WI stays open on the tracker for maintainer acceptance; the in-tree criteria are met

The gate is restored on rootful with exactly two accepted owners: the tenant root's uid (fresh leaf) and this lane's container uid (a leaf from an earlier rootful call). Any other owner is refused before the chown. Rootless keeps the strict single-owner gate
    - **NO-4 (Low, tests — fixed):** the existing pins could not see either defect — their tenant IS the invoking account, `sudo` runs fixture commands directly and `chown` is a shell no-op, so ownership never changes and the tenant chmod always succeeds.     - **Kimi runner: the credential gate is enforced, not assumed.** `task_runner.ts` extracted `main()`'s startup gates into `startupRefusal()` and added a credential gate: the runner refuses to start when `AGENT_AUTH_TOKEN` or `AGENT_AUTH_TOKEN_FILE` is present and non-empty in its own environment. The entrypoint already unset both before the exec, but that was unenforced — the runner is the env store for task code, so an inherited name would have reached every task it spawns. The gate sits **before** the uid gate so a root launch that also leaked the token reports the actionable configuration error, and so the gate is testable in both uid shapes (the image gate runs as root, where the uid gate would answer first)
    - **opencode v1 and opencode2: no task grant entries are merged into the bridge's `process.env` (run-209 NU-1).** The first cut dropped `AGENT_AUTH_TOKEN`/`AGENT_AUTH_TOKEN_FILE` before injection, but the review showed the threat description was wrong — the serve process and its bash children do **not** inherit the bridge env, and the injected value is the agent-chosen grant, not the bridge's token — and that the injection still changed shared bridge lazy reads (task identity, serve auth, retry/heartbeat/abort knobs, tool-proxy timeout). The mutation is now removed entirely rather than growing the denylist. The per-task request map remains for v1's serve-log scrub (run-92 FJ-6). Grant names are agent-chosen at `create_job` (`options.credential_grants[].env_var`); on these lanes they do not reach task code at all, so opencode credential grants are currently ineffective (follow-up **WI#474**, pending live-tenant confirmation)
    - **Pebble needed no change:** `spawn_pebble`'s `BRIDGE_RESERVED_ENV_KEYS` already covers both names. The `WORKER-CONTAINERS.md` bullet named a helper that does not exist (`strip_bridge_credentials`); it is `strip_bridge_reserved_env`, and the bullet now says so and names the four keys it filters
    - **Pins:** kimi `task_runner_test.ts` — the `presentCredentialKeys` contract, the refusal through `startupRefusal()` and `main()`, and the gate order (socket → credential → uid). Both opencode lanes — the env sampled **at prompt time**, asserting that no grant entries (reserved names or an ordinary key) land in the shared bridge env and that no env-drop path remains. **Mutation-verified:** emptying the kimi leaked list fails **three** runner pins (contract, `main()`, and gate-order), not the two the earlier trail claimed (run-209 NU-2); disabling the opencode env-isolation fails that lane's prompt-time snapshot assertion
    - **Verification on this tree:** kimi `npm test` **171 pass / 0 fail / 1 skip** (CI's node lane; `tsc` clean first); opencode2 8/8 bun suites; opencode v1 8/8 bun suites; `test-worker-llm-entrypoints.sh` **34 pass / 0 fail**; `test-mt-admin-kimi.sh`, `test-mt-admin-opencode2.sh`, `test-mt-admin-worker-image-contract.sh` all pass; `validate_config.py` rc 0 (8 YAML files); `.gitlab/tests/test_goal1_ci` **251 OK**; `shell-syntax.sh` 255 scripts + 3 hooks clean; `python_syntax.py` 133 files clean. No daemon/bridge Rust touched, so no `ic/` cargo lanes run. **Deploy note:** the kimi and both opencode changes need rebuilt worker images (protected-pipeline gate)
    - **FOLLOW-UP 2026-10-24 (run-209 review, note #11581):** NU-1 — removed the `process.env` mutation on both opencode lanes instead of reserving every lazy-read name, and filed **WI#474** for the consequence that opencode `credential_grants` never reach task code (live-tenant confirmation and a deliberate delivery/rejection design still needed). NU-2 — corrected the docs and this trail to say grant names are agent-chosen at `create_job`, removed the false "serve and every bash child inherit the bridge env" rationale, cited CI's **171 pass / 0 fail / 1 skip** for kimi, and corrected the mutation count to **three** failing runner pins (contract, `main()`, gate-order).
